\label{app:association-results}

This section reports every estimable numerical form of the indicators in Section~\ref{app:indicators}: 4,191 associations with a decision change and 2,211 associations with signed duration change. The latter equals the full rescheduling solution minus its reference duration; negative values indicate shortening. All estimates use the 75 training and test scenarios. Strategy-2 uses Strategy-1 as its reference; Strategy-3 uses the full Strategy-2 reference.

The five catalog categories are retained. Within each category and decision type, numerical forms are ordered by the absolute scenario-weighted Spearman correlation with the change indicator, retaining the correlation sign. This is a descriptive ordering of association strength, not a ranking of causal effects or importance inside STAT or ML. The standardized regression effect answers a different question: the change in the response associated with one standard deviation of the indicator. Numerical forms that coincide under this design remain separately identified; their repeated associations are not independent evidence.

In duration tables, the upper line in each numerical cell concerns the probability of a change, and the lower line concerns its signed size. Effects are in percentage points on the upper line and minutes on the lower line. Order tables have one line only. Confidence intervals account for dependence within scenarios and repeated trains, including order pairs sharing a train. Adjusted p-values use the Benjamini--Yekutieli correction separately for each strategy, decision type and response. Scientific e notation is used for small p-values: 1.1e-7 denotes $1.1\times10^{-7}$. Direction stability reports the percentages of estimates obtained by omitting one start-time group and one blockage location having the full-sample coefficient sign, in that order. Missing estimates are shown as dashes. No significance filter is applied to these tables.

\subsection{Availability of numerical forms}

Availability is assessed separately for each strategy and decision type. Of the catalog items, 26 have no calculable form in the available model and data. The register includes only the 191 items with at least one estimable, nonconstant form. A missing form may be inapplicable to that decision type or undefined for all its records; missingness is not replaced by zero. Constant forms have no estimable association.

Some catalog entries are equivalent in this deterministic design. D03, D08, I05, the two I06 bounds and the three I09 quantiles all equal the known blockage duration; D20 is twice that duration. R04 and R12 give the same normalized occupation in each time window. These entries remain identifiable in the complete tables but do not represent separate sources of information.

3 & Running & 565 & 70 & 2,117 \\

\end{table}

\subsection{Stability of prediction-input selection}

Table~\ref{tab:association-selection} links the descriptive analysis to the actual prediction inputs. It reports whether at least one numerical form of a catalog item was selected in each outer training fold and in the final model fitted to all training and test scenarios. STAT and ML share these selections. The 33 listed items were selected at least once; the remaining 158 nonconstant items were not selected in any of these fits. Reference values and operational bounds are retained separately as common controls. Selection frequency measures reproducibility of input selection, not a coefficient magnitude, causal contribution or neural feature attribution.
